481 places where se-llm differs from SE, with 926 separate changes; places with exactly the same difference are shown once, with the others folded under it: 174 in the words and 752 in the markup only. Each place shows the same passage three ways as source, with markup and line breaks: the Gutenberg HTML on top, the SE XHTML in the middle and the se-llm XHTML at the bottom. SE and se-llm are highlighted character by character where they differ, tags included; a red bar marks where one side has nothing. In Gutenberg the highlight is the passage behind a text change. Next to each place, every change has its own cell: what differs, the se-llm step that made se-llm's version, and a verdict. Tags are compared by element and by epub:type, xml:lang, class and role; ids and link targets are ignored. Verdicts are a model's judgment (Claude Sonnet) against the SE Manual of Style and se-llm's keep-the-Gutenberg-reading policy; markup changes with the same tags share one verdict. They are not proof.

SE correct letter markup
The signature is small caps in Gutenberg, not bold. SE uses a span with z3998:signature, while se-llm's <b> implies bold the source does not have and breaks the manual's rule that <b> is only for real bold.

se-llm step: [Semantic] Apply em-vs-i (81 changes); [Editorial] Modernize spelling and hyphenation; [Editorial] Close some one, any one, every one, any way, with out and similar phrases

se-llm correct eoc class
The eoc class is for an abbreviation whose period ends a sentence or clause. In SE's text "H.B.M." is followed by "Vice-Consul", so eoc is not warranted. Gutenberg has only plain text with a line break after it.

Lint resolution

Result: failed. Owner and checker model: claude-opus-5-5. Repair rounds: 0. Owner calls: 3; checker calls: 1.

Why it stopped: the lint owner resubmitted checker-rejected decisions unchanged: 2dbf9737c9d41c23b7cc2a7c88efeb7da960e361fe6fba2a3b6990f036b446a1

What the independent checker rejected:

  • The only edit targets local.css, but the only file reported for this c-012 finding is preface.xhtml. Every replacement must stay within a reported file, so this edit is out of scope. The fix has to be made within preface.xhtml, or the finding must be routed to a decision that can edit the stylesheet.
  • This is a 'fix' decision, but it leaves many reported occurrences untouched, and the rationale lists them itself: chapter-5 lines 21, 29, 32, 35, 37, 54, 76; chapter-8 lines 55, 67, 90; chapter-9 lines 23, 83; chapter-12 lines 83, 97, 107; chapter-13 line 134; chapter-14 lines 85, 93; chapter-20 lines 126, 128; dedication.xhtml line 10. The finding stays unresolved. Every reported occurrence must get a trailing-<br/> removal backed by its exact text before this can be approved as a fix.
  • Removing the class only on each reported line does not resolve the finding. The evidence lists other uses of the same undefined classes that keep them in the XHTML: chapter-5 letra on lines 70 and 78; r on lines 45, 54, 73, 76, 81, 83; c on line 40; chapter-12 i0 on line 141. Separately, stripping class="i4" from the chapter-12 verse line throws away the source's indentation instead of keeping it with a defined indent class. The undefined classes must be handled at every use, and the verse indent must be preserved.
  • The <title> replacements use old strings that appear nowhere in the evidence: '<title>XIII: DR. Seward’s Diary', the 'continued</title>' strings in chapters 2/3/4/13/16/17, the titles of chapters 21, 24 and 25, and epilogue '<title>NOTE</title>'. The chapter-7 s-021 evidence shows a <title> that differs from its heading, so these guessed strings may not match. Also, chapter-24 is deliberately left flagged ('van'). That occurrence needs its own explicit keep decision, not a 'fix' that leaves it unresolved.
  • This edit-less repair depends entirely on the chapter-24 and epilogue unwrap edits in s-008 (61e0d663…), and s-008 is rejected for incompleteness. No approved fix therefore removes the <span>/<br/> from chapter-24 line 19 or epilogue line 15. Those two edits must belong to an approved FIX decision.
  • This is a 'fix' decision, but it leaves reported occurrences unedited: chapter-6 lines 79 and 82, and chapter-19 line 76. The rationale admits this. Each reported time-plus-a.m./p.m. occurrence needs a no-break space edit based on its exact text before the finding counts as resolved.
